# HANDOFF — the last thing each chat did (newest first, ten lines each)

Format: date · what was built or decided · what is verified (commands run) · what is open. Older
entries move to `archive/records/` when this file passes forty entries. The record before
2026-09-07 is `archive/records/HANDOFF-2026-07-06-to-2026-09-07.md`.

## 2026-09-25 · 20c-i (Folder B): the Leads list (branch `leads-list`)

- **Why this job:** Stage 3 item 1d (Razorpay) waits: Kd made his free Razorpay test account (Individual, unregistered; keys in git-ignored `apps/api/.env.razorpay.local`, two test calls answered), but Subscriptions answers "Unauthorized" and its Create plan "Something went wrong"; Kd asked Razorpay to switch it on. Stage 4 items 1 and 4 need his accounts, 19a–19d need Folder A jobs, so 20c. **Risky (other people's details), Opus xhigh; Kd said *"go"*;** 20c split in three (RULINGS 2026-09-25).
- **Built:** migration `0046` (`gym_leads`: one lead per email and per phone a gym; `entry_id` keyed with the gym, ON DELETE SET NULL (entry_id), moved by a merge). Routes `/v1/orgs/:gymId/leads` (list with counts, search, status, 100 a page; one; add; PATCH; DELETE; `/join`), all `members.confirm`, writes need a live plan, 10,000 leads a gym. **Joined** (`joinRule.ts`): a record sharing the email or phone AND the name is linked; another name (a family on one address), a record taken off the list, or two same-name records are shown to staff to choose; nothing shared makes a record (`placeLeadInTx`, the list's own add). A closed gym's leads go with its list. Console: **Leads** tab after Members; `Leads.jsx`, `LeadSheet.jsx`, `leadsView.js`; the phone menu scrolls sideways (7 tabs).
- **Worst thing, first tests:** a stranger, a rival owner, a member, a trainer, nobody signed in, an unticked manager: every door refused, nothing written, no name in any refusal; a rival's lead id under this gym 404; a son on his mother's email never joined as her. **Three breaks, each RED, restored sha256-identical:** a lead read by id alone (1 red), the tick loosened to `members.read` (2 red), the list without its gym (3 red).
- **Verified:** api tsc 0 · eslint 0 · shared tsc 0 · lint 0 · 232/232 · the join rule's table 18/18 · leads routes 13/13 · **whole api suite on `aihg_b`: 121 files, 3,771 passed, 1 failed** (orgs "currency with no price book", the known clash; passes alone) · web 2,778 passed + `landingRoute` 28/28 after its console-route count moved to 8; `poseAssets.contract` local Node 24 only · web eslint 0.
- **Cost at the ceiling** (10,000 leads + 2,000 records, 2,592 MHz, the api's one connection; bystander worst of 3): open Leads 25–31 ms, a chip 23–30 ms, a search 35–91 ms, no match 31–52 ms, Load more 21–37 ms, add 32–103 ms, a status tap 61–86 ms, Joined 105–106 ms. At 200 leads: reads 14–22 ms, writes 61–126 ms (a write transaction's own round trips).
- **Looked at in headless Edge** on the real app (throwaway gym, cleaned up), computer and phone width; fixed after looking: the source shown twice on a lead, the chips cut off on a phone, the phone menu crowded.
- **Kd took the three recommendations** after the other apps' help pages were shown (RULINGS 2026-09-25): the **"Happy to hear from us by email"** tick (`gym_leads.email_ok_at`, in `0046`: refused without an email, kept while the address stays, cleared when it changes; route test), **Add lead back to the list** with "… is on your leads.", and **20c-iv** (a website form) on ROADMAP. After: leads 14/14 and four neighbouring suites, 114 passed; web leads + landingRoute 45/45; tsc and eslint 0. `0046` re-applied on `aihg_b` (its leads, Kd's click-through ones included, were dropped with the table). Next in Folder B: the click-through and round one, then 20c-ii; 1d when Razorpay opens Subscriptions.
- **Round one: 3 High, 1 Low, 6 weak tests, all fixed.** H1: Load more skipped leads of the same millisecond: the cursor carries `created_at` to the microsecond (`to_char`), and the driver turned a `::timestamptz` parameter into a JavaScript Date and dropped the microseconds, so it is sent as text (probe: 0 rows below as a timestamptz parameter, 5 as text). H2: "someone new" linked or brought back a record holding exactly the lead's details: `placeLeadInTx` answers `held` and the lead gets a 409 naming it (`join_exact`). H3: a joined lead whose record was taken off said "On your list": `onList` read with every lead, the sheet says it was taken off, and Joined ("Put back") restores it. L1: a phone typed as staff type it ("07700 900456", "+44 …") is found by its digits. Tests added: same-instant and microsecond paging, the 10,000 ceiling, one worker's limit at a shared desk, two servers at once (a duplicate at the UNIQUE now answers 409 `lead_exists`). Each new test RED first; breaks of the ceiling, the write limit and both race locks RED, restored sha256-identical.
- **The re-check closed all of round one and the round** (*"Nothing Critical or High is open"*, its probe re-run on the fixed code). Its one new Low fixed after, without a further re-check: "someone new" answered 500 when more than 50 records share the lead's contact and the held one sorts past them; that record is now read on its own (`heldRecord`; test RED first, 500 → 409). ## 2026-09-25 · Stage 3 item 1c-iii (Folder B): a smaller size (branch `paddle-smaller-size`)

- **Risky (money, Paddle's replies), Opus xhigh; Kd said *"go"*** to the end of the paid month over switching at once with a credit (RULINGS 2026-09-25). **Rebuilt after his click-through** (*"the gym already paid now we are downgrading instantly ?"*): the gym keeps its whole size until the date, its members are counted then; the Plan card redesigned with one Change size button. **Then** (*"are we doing injustice to gyms"*): too many members moves the gym to the smallest size they fit, and it stays only when none does; in the last 3 days billing staff are asked in a pop-up to remove members, move to that size, or stay (RULINGS, three lines that day).
- **Built**: migration `0045` (`subscriptions.pending_plan_id/pending_from/pending_held_at/pending_warned_at`, `billing_plan_changes.members_counted`). Choosing a smaller size on a paying plan only writes it down; the worker (`applyPendingSizes`), 3 h before the next charge, counts the members under the gym's lock: too many → the smallest smaller size they fit (`smallestFittingPlan`, `requested_plan_id` kept) is made instead, or if none the size is dropped; the count kept for the card, email; they fit → the smaller limit holds for joins from then (`pending_held_at`, in every limit read) and Paddle's price changes with `do_not_bill`; after a missed renewal, with proration (a credit). A paid trial moves at once and refuses too many. Keep is `DELETE …/billing/size/pending` ("Cancel this change"). Emails (`billing/emails.ts`) through the sign-in codes' Resend sender, to the owner and staff with the billing tick. Console: the last-days pop-up (`SizeDecisionPrompt`, in the shell), Plan card headline and next payment, the member bar, the waiting change with Cancel, how many to remove and by when with "Go to members", "your size stayed"; one Change size list; "places" is "members" everywhere.
- **Worst thing, first tests**: a stranger, a member, a trainer and a manager without the tick are refused (preview, change, cancel); the gym keeps 50 places after choosing 1; decided with 3 members it stays, nobody removed, the owner and billing manager emailed once; a join made while Paddle is making the change is refused; a join first keeps the size. **Six breaks, each RED, restored sha256-identical**: members not counted (2 red), the limit not held during the switch (1), the limit held from the choice (2), the billing check skipped (1), the fallback below the members (2), the fallback never used (1). A cut-off paid-trial change was red without its fix.
- **Verified**: api tsc 0 · eslint 0 · shared tsc 0 · lint 0 · 232/232 · billing route tests 47/47 · **whole api suite on `aihg_b`: 117 files, 3,701 passed, 1 failed** (orgs "currency with no price book", the known GBP clash; 153/153 alone) · the 3 web tests of the old card wording updated.
- **Run on Kd's sandbox through the real server, both builds**: gyms paid $140.45 for 500 and chose 200, Paddle untouched and the limit still 500; the worker at the decide time moved the gym with 0 members (next bill $86.01, $79 + tax, same date, no new transaction) and kept the gym with 201 members on 500 (Paddle untouched, next bill $140.45, 201 recorded). Paddle previews a prorated downgrade as a $54.44 credit. Test plans cancelled at Paddle; throwaway owners and members deleted from `aihg_b`. Web 2,680 passed (`poseAssets.contract` local Node 24 only).
- **Cost at 2,000 members** (2,592 MHz): the member count 1.8 ms, the limit read 0.2 ms, the worker's due scan 0.2 ms (its own index). The warning pass counts members once a run for each gym with a size due within 3 days until it is warned.
- **Kd's click-through of the pop-up passed** (*"all passed"*); he asked whether "Your size stayed…" ever goes: it now shows until the payment after the one it was decided for, or until the gym changes size (test red without it). His test gym put back (practice members removed, bill date restored).
- **Round one: 3 High, 2 Low, 4 weak tests, all fixed.** H1: a fitted size whose first Paddle attempt failed forgot the size asked for (no card line, no email) — the size asked for is kept on the row (`pending_requested_plan_id`, in `0045`) and every attempt decides from it. H2: a failed attempt kept holding the smaller limit and the card headed with it — the hold is let go on every failure but a lost answer, and the card heads with the plan's own size (`planSeatCap`). H3: billing emails reached a former billing manager — recipients now pass the routes' own check (`holdsPrivilege`). L1 "1 members". L2: a smaller size chosen while Paddle's renewal was unwritten was made at once with a credit — refused (`renewing`). Its test also found the 3-day warning sent after the decision time; now only before. Five breaks RED, restored identical.
- **The re-check closed all of round one and found N1 (High), made by H2's fix**: a paid trial's smaller size was not recounted after a failed attempt let go of its hold, so it could be made with more members than it holds. Every attempt now counts, trials included; a trial's has no fallback and is dropped (test red with the old line, restored identical). N2 (Low): the card named a remove-by time already past after a failed attempt; it names none then.
- **The second re-check closed N1 and N2 and the round** (*"Nothing Critical or High is open"*); its Low (a trial's "stays on" email read as if charged now) fixed after: a trial's says "from its first payment". Verified after: billing route tests 52/52; whole api suite 119 files, 3,741 passed, 1 failed (the known GBP clash).
- **Merged on Kd's word, 2026-09-25 (PR #112)**, CI green; ROADMAP ticked (this records PR). Next in Folder B: 1d; the two extra passes (security, integrity) run over Stage 3 item 1 when 1d is built, before a real gym pays. ROADMAP 1c-iii ticks on merge. Next in Folder B: 1d; the two extra passes run over Stage 3 item 1 when 1d is built.

## 2026-09-25 · 3a-vi-b: an app member follows their record (Folder A, branch `member-follows-record`, PR #111)

- **Risky (other people's records, a rule that picks), Opus xhigh; Kd said *"go"*.** A member who joined by invitation is matched to that invitation's record (`gym_members.entry_id`) before their email or phone: the upload's preview and confirm (`reconcile`'s `onListOf`), `membersAgainstList` (the list, Remove all, the person's page, Invite), the roster's "On your list as …" (current records only), the listed stamp. No migration, no new package. Spec §11.4 "Out of 3a-vi-b".
- **Built beside it (engineering, stated to Kd):** "Put on the list" for a member whose joined record is former brings that record back instead of making a second one (which would have left them off the list); a staged upload's groups keep each row's record id, so a second look at a preview agrees with the first.
- **Worst thing, first tests** (`memberList.joinedRecord.unit`, `.routes` with real invitations on real Postgres): Emma, email changed by the gym's software, stays on the list, out of Remove all, "Uses the app", not emailed by Invite; Priya, whose record left while her son stays on her address, is off it. **Three breaks, each RED, restored sha256-identical**: the rule ignoring the link (11 red), the database read not reading it (2 red), email matching despite it (1 red).
- **Changed on purpose:** `invitations.join.routes`' Remove-all case made Wes unlisted by changing his record's email, which no longer takes a joined member off; it now marks his record former, as a confirm does.
- **Verified**: api tsc 0 · eslint 0 · shared tsc 0 · lint 0 · 232/232 · member-list + invitation suites 36 files, 1,911 passed · full api suite 119 files, 3,697 passed, 2 failed: the Wes case (fixed, 21/21 alone) and orgs "currency with no price book" (the shared-seed clash, passed alone). Second month on the real server unchanged: 3 new · 2 missing; "Surname, First" 20 updated. Looked at in headless Edge on the real app.
- **Cost** (mains, 2,592 MHz; 10,000 × 2,000, every member's email changed): master names all 2,000 members as leaving, this job 0. Preview read again 184–568 ms over ten reads (master 344 ms; one 2,377 ms stall), Remove all's group 69–124 ms (master 224 ms), confirm's bystander worst 2.5–3.8 s (master 3.5 s), staged document 2.48 MB (master 2.09 MB: the record ids). At 200: preview read 40 ms, confirm 175 ms.
- **From Kd's click-through (RULINGS 2026-09-25, *"go"*):** Members marks an app member who is off the list — amber edge, "Not on your list · taken off {day}" / "was on an earlier list" / "not on any list you have imported", "Arjun Shah on your list has the same email", and "Put back on list" / "Add to list". Three breaks RED, restored identical (always "taken off"; any record's name; the screen ignoring the reason). The roster page's bystander worst 65–89 ms against 40–66 ms without it at 10,000 × 2,000 (mains); member-list + invitation + orgs suites 37 files, 2,065 passed; web 2,638 passed (`poseAssets.contract` Node 24 only).
- **Known edge, by 3a-vi's rule (3):** another name on a joined member's lone old address is read as their record renamed, and the member follows it; the roster's "Check this is them" then flags it.
- **Kd's click-through passed** (*"all passed"*, steps 1–8 on the demo gym, the off-list mark and Put back on list included).
- **Round one: 1 High, 3 Low, 4 weak tests, all fixed.** H1 (the reviewer ran it): staff took Emma off and typed her back in with her own email, and she was named for Remove all, marked off the list, and Put back made two of her. Now a joined member whose record is off is on through a current record on their email or phone WITH THAT RECORD'S NAME (3a-vi's same-name test), in all four places; Priya stays off (Arjun's name differs). L1: a grouping staged without record ids is worked out again. L2: "Put back on list" only for their own record taken off. L3: "taken off" only for the joined record. Six breaks RED, restored identical. Suites 37 files, 2,074 passed. With all 2,000 joined records off and copies on: Remove all 116 ms, the list 129 ms (bystander worst 96–106 ms).
- **The re-check closed the round** (all four closed, nothing Critical/High); its one new Low fixed after (the roster now says "On your list as" the typed-in record she is on the list through; red without it). **Merged on Kd's word, 2026-09-25 (PR #111)**, CI green. Next in Folder A: 5b.

## 2026-09-25 · Stage 3 item 1c-ii (Folder B): a bigger size, and paying during the trial (branch `paddle-size-and-trial-pay`)

- **Risky (money, Paddle's replies), Opus xhigh; Kd said *"go"*** to the plan and its recommendation (RULINGS 2026-09-25): a gym that pays during its free trial is charged when the trial ends. ROADMAP gains **1c-iii, a smaller size**.
- **Built**: Subscribe during the gym's own trial sells a Paddle trial of the days left (a price made for that checkout, carrying the plan's code; Paddle allows a trial only on a price) and the gym's own trial gives way to it (`cancel_reason` 'subscribed'); the rule (`machine.ts`) now writes Paddle's `trialing` (`trial_started`, `converted` on the first payment); the free-trial sweep leaves a paid trial to Paddle; `subscribed` on the gym's plan. `POST /v1/orgs/:gymId/billing/size/preview` and `/size` (Idempotency-Key): only a bigger size, recorded first under the gym's lock with one pending change a gym (migration `0044`, `billing_plan_changes`), Paddle's subscription read before any change, `prorated_immediately` with `prevent_change` (a trial: `do_not_bill`, Paddle's only mode there). Console: Choose a plan (trial) and Choose a bigger size (Plan card, and the nearly-full line on Members), one closable dialog; Subscribe's Paddle code shared with the prompt (`usePaddleSubscribe`).
- **Worst thing, first test**: two presses at once make one change and one charge; another gym's owner, a member, a trainer and a manager without the tick are refused and Paddle never asked. **Three breaks, each RED, restored sha256-identical**: a second press let through while one is pending (200, 200), Paddle not read before a retry (409 — the second guard caught it), the billing check removed (another gym's owner changed the plan, 200 not 404).
- **Run on Kd's sandbox, through the real server** (api :3001 with `.env.paddle.local`, Paddle's checkout paid in headless Edge with its test card): the window read "4 day free trial · Due today $0.00"; a trial gym got a 10-day Paddle trial at $0 and "first payment of $79"; a bigger size in the trial charged nothing and Paddle kept the trial and its date; a paying gym's preview said $130.65 ($120 + $10.65 tax) and **two presses at once made one change, one $130.65 charge**. All test subscriptions cancelled at Paddle; throwaway owners deleted from `aihg_b`.
- **Verified**: api tsc 0 · eslint 0 · shared tsc 0 · eslint 0 · 232/232 · the five billing files 197/197 (29 route tests, the rule's table 144) · **whole api suite on `aihg_b`: 115 files, 3,635 passed, 1 failed** (the privacy guard: `billing_plan_changes` not on a list — added, 27/27 after) · web 2,658 passed (`poseAssets.contract` local Node 24 only) · changed web files lint 0. Cost: reads a member count only.
- **Kd's click-through passed** (*"all passed"*) and found that a paid trial opened the chosen size at once; Kd chose **A** (RULINGS 2026-09-25): a trial keeps its 200 until the first payment. `subscriptions.trial_seat_cap` (in `0044`; `aihg_b`'s ledger hash re-stamped) holds it; the limit in force is the smaller of it and the plan's while trialing (`seatCapFor` and the three plan reads); `nextSeatCap` tells the console. Two breaks RED, restored identical (the limit read off the plan alone: 5000 not 200; the trial limit never written). Re-run on the sandbox: a trial gym choosing 500 holds at 200 with 500 next; a paying gym moves to 1,000 at once, one $130.65 charge. Whole api suite: 115 files, 3,636 passed · web 2,663 passed.
- **Round one: 2 High, 2 Low, 4 weak tests, all fixed.** H1: a paid trial whose first charge failed opened the chosen size for the grace — the trial's limit now holds until a payment is taken (`LEAST` whatever the status; cleared on `active`). H2: a trial checkout paid late (or rounded up) gave free days past the gym's own trial — each Paddle trial is moved to the gym's own trial end, or activated at once if that has passed (both calls run first on Kd's sandbox). ## 2026-09-25 · 3a-vi: the same person next month (Folder A, branch `same-person-next-month`)

- **Risky (other people's records, a rule that picks), Opus xhigh; Kd said *"go and i agree recommendation"*** — the recommendation being 3a-vi-b, an app member matched by the invitation they joined with, as its own job before 5b (RULINGS 2026-09-25, ROADMAP).
- **Built**: `samePerson.ts` (`matchRows`, pure): all carried fields equal, else member number, else email (the name picks on a shared one), else phone; one row to one record; never one person on a different name AND different number/contact, or a different date of birth. `reconcile` uses it; the confirm finds a record by its old key and writes the new name, email, phone, member number and key; the four identity fields are hand-edit marks too. No migration, no new package. Spec §11.4 "Out of 3a-vi".
- **Outside cases** (a research pass, quotes in the test's header): PushPress and zingFit on families sharing a parent's email; Mindbody's editable client ID (SOK1234 → XSOK1234); GymMaster's reassigned key fobs; FCC's 35 million reassigned numbers a year; Zen Planner's export with no ID column. Two of them changed the rule before review (a member number alone neither joins nor parts two people).

Spec §10.2 "Out of 3b-ii-a" has the details.
- **Found on the way, fixed**: `POST /v1/auth/register` let anybody plant a password account on an address they do not hold; the first proof now ends any password and session set before it, and an invitation opens only to a session begun after the proof (access tokens carry `fid`). - **Not this job's, fixed here**: 3b-i's invitation tests failed now and then on CI only ("two workers at once" left a report pending; a send not yet due). Cause, reproduced locally by forcing it: the database stamps microseconds and a JS Date keeps whole milliseconds, so a clock read in the same millisecond as a row's `now()` reads earlier than it (stamp .394595, clock .394). The tests' worker clocks now run one millisecond ahead; in production such a row waits one worker run. Two class tests that failed here on a Wednesday evening, London time, were fixed by Folder B's #101.
- **Cost** (mains, 2,592 MHz): Join 58–69 ms median at 20×200 and at the cap; what is waiting 10–13 ms; Remove all of 1,000 with withdrawals 311 ms median (one Docker-disk stall at 1,168 ms).

## 2026-09-23 · 3b-i-b: what comes back (Folder A, branch `invite-results`, PR #100)

- **Built** (risky, Opus xhigh; Kd said *"go"*, nothing new decided, so no RULINGS line): `POST /v1/webhooks/resend` (Svix signature on the raw bytes, 5-minute window, kept once by `svix-id`; only type · Resend's id · the `invite_send` tag · bounce type and sub-type stored). Every invitation email carries the tag, so a report finds its row even before Resend's id is known. The worker's `invites.results` job acts on a report only after `GET /emails/{id}` agrees (same tag, a matching last event); a hard bounce keeps the address from every gym, a complaint from that gym, and an address on Resend's own list (`refused`) from every gym without counting against the gym. A gym's first 50 then wait (all back, or an hour); it stops past 2 % hard bounces (of emails that went, counted as at least 50) or on a complaint in its first 100, its waiting emails skipped as `sending_stopped`; `OPERATOR_EMAIL` told once; `tools/invites-stopped.ts` lists and resumes. Migration `0039`. No new package. Spec §9.12 "Out of 3b-i-b".
- **The worst thing, first tests**: a report nobody at Resend signed changes nothing; a signed one Resend's record does not bear out stops nobody; after a confirmed complaint that gym cannot send again; after a confirmed hard bounce no gym invites the address. **Three breaks of the core rule, each RED, restored sha256-identical** (re-run on the final code): any signature accepted; the confirmation skipped; the complaint's suppression dropped.
- **Outside cases**: Svix's published test vector; Resend's published `email.bounced` example (a `Suppressed` bounce, read as refused); the confirmation table derived from the event orders on Resend's "Event Types" page, not from the code's list.
- **Verified**: api tsc 0 · api eslint 0 · shared tsc 0 · shared eslint 0 · shared 220/220 · the four invite files together 301/301 · full api suite on local Postgres 105 files, 3,279 passed, 0 failed · web: `console.render` and `nutritionChangeLogged.render` each failed once in the full run under load and passed alone (132/132); `poseAssets.contract` fails locally on Node 24 only, as before. No harness targets a file this job changed.
- **Cost** (mains, 2,592 MHz): a report arriving 36 ms median (224 worst), mostly the commit; in the worker, the gate 9 ms at the launch shape and 35–37 ms with a gym of 10,000 sent; a report at that gym 125–134 ms, a same-gym list write waiting 60–61 ms median (158 worst).

## 2026-09-23 · 17b-ii-b-ii-a (Folder B): a time slot changed from a date

- **17b-ii-b-ii was split first**: **-a** here (a time slot changed from a date), **-b** next (Edit all time slots of a class at once, TeamUp's Bulk Edit). Risky job, Opus xhigh. Branch `class-slot-from-date`. Kd said *"go"* to the plan; RULINGS 2026-09-23 records the one behaviour he agreed to (a move replaces classes changed or cancelled on their own, and asks first).
- **What staff see**: Classes → a time slot's **Edit** has Days, Start time and **Update from** (starts at its next class that has not run); Calendar → a class's **Edit** asks **This class only** · **This and future classes**. A move that would replace classes changed or cancelled on their own asks "2 classes from Mon 5 Oct on were changed or cancelled on their own. Move anyway?" — **Move anyway** · **Go back**. "Next:" starts at now.
- **Server**: the time slot PUT takes `updateFrom`, `weekdays`, `startMinute`; the class PUT takes `scope`. One pure rule (`classes/slotRule.ts`, 30-row table test) says keep · restamp · replace for each coming class; the repo writes by the ids it answers, under the gym's lock, every check before the first write. A move ends the time slot the day before (or stops it when nothing is left before the date) and starts a new one; a new length, size or coach splits it at the date when it still runs before it, the classes moving with their ids. 409 `class_slot_replaces` carries the count until the request confirms the same number. A finished time slot no longer counts to the cap. No migration.
- **The worst thing, and the core rule broken three ways, each RED, files restored identical to the commit**: the rule without its before-the-date check (table rows and the worst route test red), a move deleting every coming class (*"expected [] to deeply equal [10 rows]"*), the split moving classes from today (*schedule_id* changed on classes before the date).

## 2026-09-23 · 17b-ii-b-i (Folder B): the week view, and one day changed or cancelled

- **17b-ii-b was split first** (week view, one-day changes, "this day and later" and the bulk edit is more than one terminal): **17b-ii-b-i** here, **17b-ii-b-ii** next. Risky job, Opus xhigh. Branch `class-week-view`. Kd said go to the plan; he decided nothing new, so no RULINGS line.
- **What staff see**: Classes → **Week** tab (in the address, `?view=week`): Monday to Sunday in the gym's calendar, seven columns on a computer and a list by day on a phone, arrows up to the last fully written week, filters by class and coach. Tap a class: **Change this day** (time, length, places, coach), **Cancel this day** (asks first; stays on the week crossed out), **Put it back on**. A started class offers nothing.
- **Server**: `GET /v1/orgs/:gymId/class-sessions?week=`, `PUT …/class-sessions/:id`, `POST …/cancel` and `…/restore`, each `schedule.manage`, the pair in every WHERE, the coach checked under the gym's lock. The rule of what one date may take is pure (`classes/dayRule.ts`) with a 26-row table test. No migration. A cancel is a STATUS, not `changed_alone` (spec §13.3 note): nothing that writes the calendar touches status, so a put-back day runs as its repeat then does. **One class, one time, one date**: a day cannot move or be put back onto a time the class already runs, and the fill writes no second one there.
- **The worst thing, first test**: a cancelled class coming back (the fill run twice, the nightly job, a repeat edit) or another gym cancelling it by id. **Three breaks, all red, files restored sha256-identical**: the fill as an upsert (*"expected 'scheduled' to be 'cancelled'"*), a repeat edit resetting status (same), the gym dropped from the day's key (*"expected 200 to be 404"*).

Changing a day 46.8 ms median, cancelling 36.0 ms (whole transaction). The fill's new check first cost a normal night +54 %; checking the repeat's own date first put it back level — **12 nights interleaved: master 1,524 ms, this 1,386 ms** for 20 gyms.

## 2026-09-22 · 3a-v-b: the gym's whole file is kept, and nobody is deleted from the list (PR #92)

- **Built** (Folder A, risky, Opus xhigh; spec §11.1, §11.4, §11.5). Migration `0037` (written as `0035`, renumbered as Folder B's jobs merged first): nine columns on an entry (membership and payment words, joined, ends and birth dates, end-or-renewal kind, the gym's own columns as one document, `former_at`, hand-edited field names) and `gym_member_list_fields`, the gym's catalogue of its own headings, 40 at most.
- **Nobody is deleted.** A person the file no longer holds becomes former; the upload that holds them again revives the same row. Former records are left out of the list, the chips and `canBeInvited`, and out of a page unless `records=former|all` asks for them.
- **Changes are counted field by field**, a field the file has no column for is left alone, and the gym's own columns merge by key. A hand edit is never overwritten without its own tick (409 `hand_edits`, a count and field names only); a refused confirm writes nothing.
- **Card numbers (§11.2).** A whole-cell card is dropped as before; a card inside a name, a word, a sample or one of the gym's columns becomes `[card number removed]` (issuer prefix + card grouping + Luhn), asked again where the confirm writes. 546/546 test cards caught, 0 of 12 ordinary cells and 0–1 % of random number lists altered, 0.05 ms a 500-character cell.
- **Logs** keep a request's path and drop its query string, which had written a searched member's address into the log.
- **Cost at full size (§9.9, on mains)**, 10,000 people × 30 columns: a page 66–87 ms, the confirm 2,270–3,040 ms (was 980–1,635); the launch shape is unchanged at 128–231 ms. Such a file is about 4 MB of the 5 MiB cap.
- **Verified after taking in master**: api tsc 0 · api eslint 0 · shared tsc 0 · shared eslint 0 · api suite on local Postgres and Redis 95 files, 2,783 passed · shared 213 passed · census 65/65. Breaks 43–64 each RED before the merge.
- **Review round closed** (`reviews/3a-v-b-4-verdict.md`). The whole-cell rule's missing issuer-prefix check is filed on ROADMAP item 10.
- **Kd decided** (RULINGS 2026-09-22): who counts as the same person does not change, so a corrected spelling leaves a former record beside the new one; the person's page (3a-iv) is where to join them.
- **Open**: PR #92 waits for Kd's "merge". Next in Folder A: 3a-iv. The four dashboard images on disk are nobody's.

- **What a gym can now do**: each repeat carries its own length, places and coach, shown beside it (*Thu at 18:00 · 90 min · No limit · Priya Sharma*), filled in from the class when the repeat is added and changeable afterwards. The class's three numbers are labelled for what they now are — **"A new repeat starts from: 60 min · 20 places · Priya Sharma"** — because since Kd's ruling they are not what the class runs as.
- **Editing a class changes no repeat and no date already on the calendar.** That is the reversal this card is, and it is TeamUp's own split, read on the build day and further than the ruling needed: a Class Type there edits *"the class name, description, and visibility"* while a time slot edits *"the Venue, Instructor, Times, and Class Size Limits"*. Editing a REPEAT re-stamps its coming dates and leaves the past and a `changed_alone` day alone. Moving a repeat's day or time is deliberately absent — that is "this day and later", 17b-ii-b, and the form says so.
- **New**: migration `0036` (three columns on `gym_class_schedules`, back-filled from each repeat's class, then `minutes` made NOT NULL); `PUT /v1/orgs/:gymId/class-repeats/:scheduleId`; the fill stamps a new date from the REPEAT, never the type; `gym_class_schedules` joins `USER_LINKED_NOT_PURGED_TABLES` (its old entry said a repeat "carries no user link at all", which stopped being true); `RunFields` draws the three fields once for all three forms.
- **The worst thing, and the first test written**: a gym's timetable naming somebody who is not that gym's staff as the coach of Tuesday's class. `coach_user_id` is a FK to `users`, so nothing structural refuses another gym's trainer — `coachIsStaff` inside every write, under the gym's lock, is what does. One test asks all three doors (a class, a new repeat, a repeat being changed) with four kinds of outsider, and checks the ROWS, not the replies.
- **Three deliberate breaks of that one rule, all RED, control GREEN, `repo.ts` restored sha256-identical**: the coach check dropped from the create (*"another gym's trainer was accepted by …/repeats: expected 201 to be 400"*), dropped from the edit (*"…/class-repeats/…: expected 200 to be 400"*), and the edit's `gym_id` dropped from its key (*"expected 200 to be 404"*). No harness covers this module, so they were made by hand with the harnesses' own discipline.
- **A defect the tests found while this was being written, and it is 17b-i's too**: the coach box is a `<select>`, and a value not among its options renders BLANK and the next Save sends `null`. Two ordinary cases reach it — the staff list is a separate optional read that 403s for somebody who may set the timetable but not manage staff, and a coach who has left is answered as an id with no name — so a gym would lose its coach with nobody touching that box. Whoever is set is now always an option, named where the server still names them and **"No longer on your staff"** where it does not; the repeat line says *"Coach not on this gym's staff"* rather than nothing.
- **Proven by running**: api tsc 0 · api eslint `src test tools` 0 · shared tsc 0 · shared eslint 0 · shared 202/202 · engine 213/213 · engine purity grep silent · **the whole api suite on `localhost:5433/aihg_b` with the real Redis: 93 files, 2,666 passed, 0 failed** · web 2,408 passed (`poseAssets.contract.test.js` fails locally on Node 24 and is green on CI's Node 22 — pre-existing) · gitleaks clean.
- **Driven through the real server and a real browser**, not only by `inject`: signed in on `127.0.0.1:3001` with the real CORS origin, added a repeat that differs from its class, edited the class and watched the repeat and its 8 dates NOT move, edited the repeat and watched all 8 follow, and had a coach from no gym at all refused 400 `coach_not_staff`. Headless Edge drove the screen end to end: the pre-filled form, the repeat's own line, and the edit form with no day or time on it.
- **Cost at full size** (2026-09-22, 2,592 MHz, on mains — checked), 20 gyms × 60 classes all running daily = 68,400 dates: the migration's back-fill is **69 ms for 1,200 repeats**, once; **changing a repeat is 47.0 ms median / 71.4 p95 / 143.4 worst** for the whole transaction, re-stamping **56 dates**, which is the most any one repeat can own; the api's event loop is blocked **0.96 ms** by the read's shaping (36.3 KB). One 3,496 ms read in the first run never recurred in 50 further samples — a Docker-volume stall, not a code path.
- **Records**: ROADMAP 17b-i ticked (its records were written before Kd merged it) and its story archived; 17b-ii split into a and b; the 2026-09-22 ruling re-pointed at 17b-ii-a. Kd decided nothing new, so no new RULINGS line.
- **Kd clicked through it and passed it, then asked the two questions that matter**: is "editing the class leaves the repeats alone" what real products do, and what happens if he adds a repeat again. Read that day, and four of five products do exactly what shipped — TeamUp's class type holds only name, description and visibility; PushPress asks "this class only / this class and future classes on this day / this class and all future classes in the series" and says *"other times in the series are not affected"*; Gymdesk has no class type at all; Mindbody asks for how long. Momence is the one that pushes a template down — and it ASKS. Several repeats of one class is the normal shape everywhere (Momence: *"click Add interval"*).
- **One gap that reading found, and Kd took the recommendation** (*"i agree with your recommendation"*, RULINGS 2026-09-22): nothing changes ALL the repeats of one class at once, so Yoga running five times a week with a new coach is five edits. TeamUp's Bulk Edit does it, filtered, and *"doesn't change past classes"*. It goes into **17b-ii-b**, not here — this diff is green and waiting on its review, and a second way to write would re-open it.
- **Round one: nothing Critical or High, four Lows, all fixed** — the fill's own suite could not tell the repeat's numbers from its class's; the removed-classes list still printed the bare line; a new repeat filled in a coach who had left and was refused; the audit could not say the coach changed. Each fix shown red without itself; the re-check closed all four the same way. **Merged on Kd's word, 2026-09-22 (PR #93).**
- **Next in Folder B**: 17b-ii-b — the week view, this day only · this day and later, moving a repeat's day or time, and changing every repeat of one class at once. The four dashboard images on disk are still nobody's.

## 2026-09-22 · 17b-i (Folder B): a gym says what it runs and when it repeats, and the calendar writes itself eight weeks ahead

- **The 17b line was split first** (three tables, the repeat rule, a nightly worker AND a week calendar is more than one terminal): **17b-i** here, **17b-ii** next — the week view and changing a single day. PR **#91**, three commits, CI green.
- **What a gym can do**: console → **Classes**. Add a class (name, minutes, places or "no limit", a coach from its own staff, a colour, an open-gym tick), edit it, remove it, bring it back, and say when it repeats. **Saving a repeat writes its dates at once** and shows the next four.
- **The one rule, in the database**: a repeat is the gym's CLOCK TIME plus `gyms.timezone`, and each date's instant is derived from the pair. Measured: 18:00 London is `17:00+00` on 29 Mar 2026 and `18:00+00` on 25 Oct. The gap hour and the doubled hour both keep their class. New: migration `0035`, a tenth privilege `schedule.manage` (owner and manager only), `modules/orgs/classes/*`, a nightly job at 05:00 UTC, `shared/classes.ts`, the Classes screen. `TimePick` moved out of `OpeningHoursPanel` rather than written twice.
- **Kd clicked through it, passed it, and changed three things.** (1) *"Will gyms set things 8 weeks ahead?"* — the sentence made him think so and no real product explains its own generation window; struck, and with it the date COUNT on an open-ended repeat, which was the window's size and not how often the class runs. (2) **Bring back** a removed class — Mindbody restores an archived one, TeamUp cannot, and he took Mindbody's. (3) **Nothing that destroys dates does it on one tap**: Remove and Stop both ask in place (`ConfirmInline`, new in `ConsoleStates`), never over the page.
- **Round one found three Critical/High, every one by RUNNING it, none visible to the suites.** **C/H-1**: the nightly fill took no lock, so a Stop, a Remove or an Edit committing while it ran left 56–560 coming dates on the calendar and 560 sessions holding the old length — all three screens telling the gym the opposite. The job now fills **one gym at a time under that gym's row lock**. **C/H-2**: one `LIMIT` covered the live and archived lists, so a gym with 130 removed classes saw 117 and could not bring back the other 13 — two statements now, with the gym's real total. **C/H-3**: Kd's own catch a second time — a repeat ending a year out runs 52 times and read "8 dates on the calendar"; the SERVER now says whether the count is the whole truth (`datesComplete`), and the page prints no number when it is not.
- **Seven Lows, all fixed**: a finished repeat said "nothing on the calendar **yet**" (`finished`, also the server's); a test that was wrong one run in sixteen; the seven client calls had no URL or verb test; the same repeat was saveable twice (now a 409 on an overlap); the fill's cost measured on the wrong axis; this entry's own length; and the §13.3 field dropped without a ruling — **the only thing still open, and it is Kd's**.
- **The three weak tests the review named are gone.** The first race test PASSED with the lock removed — written with `Promise.all` and hoping; it now holds the console write open until the job is in flight, and goes red with **56 dates left**, the reviewer's own number. Each C/H fix was broken on purpose and each guard bit.
- **Cost, on the axis §4 asks for** (how long the server answers nobody), 20 gyms × 60 classes running daily = 68,400 dates: a console write on that gym is **57.7 ms quiet and 70.5 ms median / 276.8 ms worst while the fill runs**, against the reviewer's pre-fix **2,726 ms**. The api's own event loop is blocked 1.2 ms by the read's shaping.
- **Proven by running**: api tsc/lint 0 · shared 193/193 · engine 213/213 · **the whole api suite 90 files / 2,300 passed** · web **2,391 passed**. `poseAssets.contract.test.js` fails locally on Node 24 and is green on CI's Node 22 — pre-existing, reproduced on a clean tree.
- **Low-7 is closed by Kd, against the card**: asked for the recommendation and the industry standard, he ruled **follow the standard**. Read that day — TeamUp edits venue, class size limit, start/end time and instructors PER TIME SLOT; Mindbody changes a teacher for one day, a period or permanently — so a REPEAT owns its coach, places and length and the class type is the default it starts from. RULINGS 2026-09-22; it is the FIRST half of 17b-ii, not a re-opening of this diff.
- **The re-check closed both other Criticals by DRIVING them — and found the same falsehood a third time, one line lower.** `repeatFactsLine` had stopped printing the count; `nextDatesLine` underneath it still printed **"+4 more"** beside **"ongoing"** about a class that runs 52 times, and this card's test pinned that as correct just as the struck one had. `+N more` is now gated on the same `datesComplete`, and says **"more to come"** otherwise. Removing the gate turns both the unit and the render case red; the real browser reads *"From Tue 22 Sep 2026 · ongoing / Next: … · more to come"*.
- **THE ROUND CLOSED on `a277354`** in that same reviewer terminal — nothing Critical or High open, every shape of repeat read out of the real server, and each closure driven with a negative control (the still-exported lock-free statement still leaves 56 dates, so the lock is what changed the outcome). Its one parting line, raised for 17b-ii rather than as a finding, is fixed here instead: `nextDatesLine` still said "No dates **yet**." under a repeat that had finished — Low-1's own word surviving in the other function, which is the class rather than the case. A re-check's Low is fixed and merged without asking (RULINGS 2026-09-16).
- **Next**: **merge** on Kd's word, then 17b-ii — which now leads with §13.3's coach and places moving onto the repeat.
## 2026-09-22 · 3a-v-a: the wider member row is READ, and §11.2's never-keep rules run inside the file worker (PR #90)

- **The job** (Folder A's first, Opus xhigh, risky). Spec §11.1–11.3, the reading only — no database, no route, no screen. Beside the five things a list kept, a gym's file now gives the **membership type**, the **join date**, the **end-or-renewal date** (with which of the two its heading said), the **payment word** and the **date of birth**; every OTHER column is kept under the gym's own heading as that gym's extra field, at most 40, each cell cut at 500 characters and counted. New files: `memberList/neverKeep.ts` and `memberList/dates.ts`.
- **Kd's one decision, recorded (RULINGS 2026-09-22).** A postcode is kept in EVERY country's word for one, not India's alone — postcode, post code, postal code, ZIP, Zip/Postal Code, PIN Code, Pincode, Eircode. A **bare "PIN"** is the heading no word list can settle, so the SHEET settles it: a postcode where the sheet carries an address column, a door code where it carries none; a heading that says it is a key ("Door PIN") is dropped either way.
- **The rules came from outside the code, as §4 demands.** Read 2026-09-22: Gymdesk's "Data Imports Overview" (its own field list, including "Zip/Postal Code" and "Medical Conditions"), its payment-migration page, GymMaster's data-transfer page, and Wikipedia's "Postal code". The tests include headings no list of ours holds — "Locker No", "Batch", "Gotra" — because a table built from the code's own words proves only that the words match themselves.
- **Every self-checking number is proved against its own standard's vectors**, not against this code: Luhn and the schemes' published test cards, Verhoeff's own 236→3 and 12345→1, the IBAN registry's three examples, and HMRC's rule that **QQ123456C is its own INVALID placeholder** (my first test vector was wrong, not the code). **Measured here: 7,269 of 100,000 made-up twelve-digit runs pass Aadhaar's Verhoeff check** — so every shape but the payment card is asked of a whole COLUMN and never of one cell, or a gym's twelve-digit member numbers would be thrown away.
- **THREE FAULTS THE NEW TESTS CAUGHT WHILE THIS WAS BEING WRITTEN, ALL OF THE WORST-THING CLASS.** (1) A never-kept column was still taken back **on its CELLS** and read as the member's **phone number** — a bank account number and an Aadhaar are both about twelve digits, which is a possible phone, so "Bank Account Number" became `+9112345678` on a member's record. Skipping those columns while reading the HEADINGS was not enough. (2) A card-shaped cell still appeared among the **three sample cells shown to staff**, though the row itself had dropped it — a card on the screen is a card kept. (3) The cheap gate before the ID checks demanded nine digits, which skipped India's PAN (four digits) and the UK's NI number (six) **entirely**.
- **Dates are settled ONCE for a whole column and never cell by cell**: staff's switch, else the file's own evidence (a cell whose first or second part is over 12), else the gym's country — and the reading is said back with a real cell beside it ("we read 03/04/2026 as 2026-04-03"). A cell that is no date is left empty and counted. Two-digit years follow Excel's own pivot (00–29 this century, 30–99 the last), which is what lets the file have **no clock** in it.
- **Seven deliberate breaks of §11.2, all RED**, and they are now rows 31–37 of `apps/api/tools/mutate-member-list.mjs` rather than a scratchpad, because 3a-v-b and the feature's two hard passes read this same rule. The harness gained a `pure` flag so a mutant whose suite needs no database does not boot Postgres. `--census` **38/38 anchors present** before and after; every file restored and sha256-checked.
- **Verified: api tsc 0 · api eslint `src test tools` 0 · shared tsc 0 · shared eslint 0 · the whole api suite on the local Postgres and the real Redis 2,515 passed, 91 files, 0 failed** · 239 new tests across `memberList.wider`, `memberList.neverKeep` and `memberList.dates` · gitleaks clean. Six older tests changed, each because this card REVERSES what they asserted (the join date and the "Paid" column were kept from nobody before), and each now says what it was really for.
- **Cost at full size, into spec §9.9** (2026-09-22, 2,592 MHz, on mains — checked, a battery halves it). 10,000 people × 30 of the gym's own columns: **696 ms in the worker** (nobody waits; the parse timeout is 15,000 ms), **9.2 MB crossing back** where it was 3.2, and **118 ms the request's thread answers nobody**, once per upload, where it was 44. At the launch shape of 200 it is **4 ms**; at a band-5 gym of 2,100, 34 ms. The 9.2 MB is the number to watch, not the milliseconds: a staged upload's row grew threefold, and 3a-v-b measures it again once that row is written per entry.
- **Round one found three Criticals and all three were cells this card KEPT that §11.2 forbids** — a card typed with spaces kept as the member number, a guardian’s address read as the member’s own because "Fathers Name" has no apostrophe where "Father’s Name" does, and a US gym’s door PIN kept on every member because the sheet had an address. Two Highs (the market lists held only the long form, and a date column’s order came from its first 200 cells) and two Lows. **All seven fixed and driven; the suite is 2,607 and the breaks are 41.** The review also named six tests that would have stayed green and was right about every one — including harness row 37, which broke the PIN rule into a behaviour the shipped code already had.
- **THE ROUND IS CLOSED**, over two re-checks in the same reviewer terminal. Round one: 3 Criticals, 2 Highs, 2 Lows. **Two of the three faults the re-checks then found were made by my own fixes** — making the PIN rule fire only on a heading that IS "PIN" left "Member PIN" and "Check-in PIN" kept on every sheet, and matching plurals to catch "Fathers Name" threw away "Fins" as a Singapore ID and "GPS Watch" as a doctor. The PIN rule is now three branches and no new word list, because a list failed there twice: an ADDRESS-PLACE qualifier is a postcode, the bare word is settled by the sheet, any other qualifier is a key. A DWELLING ("Flat PIN") is a key, though it still counts towards whether the sheet carries an address at all. PR #90 is green and not merged — Kd has not said "merge", and the ROADMAP line’s merge date assumes today. Then 3a-v-b (keeping it: one migration, the reconcile rule over the wider row, former records, the filters). The four dashboard images on disk are still nobody’s.

## 2026-09-21 · 3a-iii-b: the re-check closed both Highs and found a third that my own fix had made

- **High-3, and it was mine, an hour old.** Low-5's fix put a ceiling on the status chips; `readList` went on building the whole-list `counts` by adding those capped rows up — which is exactly what the comment directly above that loop existed to prevent. Past 200 distinct status words a gym read **"200 people" above a list of 205**, and `canBeInvited` — the number 3b's Invite button acts on — was short by everyone in the truncated groups.
- Driven before touching anything: 205 entries carrying 205 distinct words read back `entries: 200`. Fixed by summing the totals INSIDE the statement, in a CTE before the cut, in the shape `entriesPage` already used — the chips stay capped, `counts` is the whole list, and one statement still answers both.
- **The claim I wrote with the cap was the error underneath the bug**: that `counts` is measured "by a different statement". There is no different statement, and adding one would be the two-answers-to-one-question the single query exists to avoid. Struck.
- A file cannot carry that many words — a column with more than twenty is not a status (§9.5) — so the test builds the state the way a real gym reaches it: entries accumulated, which `add` uploads do a score at a time and 3a-iv's typed person one at a time. **Nothing had covered the cap at all.**
- **The anchor census earned its place again**: this fix moved the chips' ORDER BY into a new lateral and rotted break #21, which would have run GREEN and read as a rule that holds. Repointed; 31/31 anchors present.
- The round's other findings all closed: both earlier Highs, all four Lows, and three of the four weak tests. **The fourth is left open on purpose and is written down** — the lock test's second half cannot separate the upload lock from the gym lock, because the confirm updates that same upload row before it commits, so Postgres blocks it either way. Separating them needs a seam inside the transaction. That is a judgement, not a proof, and it is in the test for the next reviewer to overrule.
- **Three defects in this feature were found by review or by measurement rather than by reading the code, and two of the three were mine**: the random list order (`0034`), the in-app question answered by the seat rule, and this one. The pattern in all three is the same — a claim in a comment that nothing drove.

- **The breaks harness is now `apps/api/tools/mutate-member-list.mjs` rather than a chat scratchpad**, which is what 3a-iii’s roadmap line asked for (“the mutation harness on the rule”) and what `mutate-orgs.mjs` does not cover — it targets `orgs/repo.ts` and `service.ts` and never reaches `memberList/`. 31 mutants, `--census` first, and the two expected greens documented at the top with the reason. 3a-iv changes this same rule and the feature’s two hard passes read it; a harness that dies with its chat protects nothing.

## 2026-09-21 · 3a-iii-b: round one's two Highs fixed, and the first one was wider than the review saw

- **High-1: the console said "not in the app" about people who are in it, and offered to invite them.** `inApp` was answered from the SEAT set — live, not complimentary, not staff — which §9.7 is right to use for the marks and wrong to use for the entry's flag. "Does this person already have the app" and "does this person hold a paid seat" are different questions. The owner, any trainer who also trains there and every complimentary member read as absent and went into `canBeInvited`, the number 3b's Invite button acts on.
- **The reviewer found it on the reads. It was also on the preview**, because `listMembers` carries the identical filter — so a gym would have been offered those invites before anybody pressed Confirm. Reproduced both halves here before touching anything: four people with accounts, all four live members, all four on the export, three of them reading as not in the app.
- Fixed as one question answered once: both statements return every LIVE member with a `seatCounted` column, "already in the app" uses all of them, and the marks, the leavers, the guard and the seat count use the seat rule. **The flip side is asserted in the same test** — letting staff into the marks is the opposite bug, and §9.7 keeps them out so a gym is never told it has lost its own owner.
- **High-2: "cost at full size" had measured one axis and called it full size.** A list holds 10,000 and a gym holds 2,100 (band 5), and `membersAgainstList` grows as members × entries — this card's own note said so. Re-measured at the crossing: a page's worst bystander wait is **69–147 ms against 11–12 ms**, `GET /` **72 ms against 18–27 ms**, and walking the list **9.4–9.8 s against 3.2 s**. §9.9 has a third column and the "cheap at both sizes" sentence is gone.
- Four Lows, all fixed: a number this same commit struck was still asserted three files away; `0034` had no migration-test block and now reads its column and index off the deployed database; `GET /`'s `statuses` had no ceiling, where `add` uploads accumulate words and 3a-iv types more in; and `apps/*/.cost/` was untracked but unignored while naming a local database in plain text — **in a public repository**.
- **The card's own hazard is now driven end to end**, which is the fix I am most glad of: a real CHECK violation raised by the confirm's own INSERT, through the real error handler, with the log captured. The line carries type, message, stack, code, severity, constraint, table and routine — and no `detail`, which is the field that holds the whole failing row. The constraint is scoped to the one gym by its id, so nothing else sharing the database can be refused by it.
- `expectApplied` is exported and pinned directly rather than claimed by a route test: no route can drive it, because the rule and the three statements share one transaction under the gym's lock and the rule folds the file's own duplicates before the INSERT sees them. The post-confirm `ANALYZE` is observed through `pg_stat_user_tables`, with the shared-database limit of that written into the test.
- **Two breaks stay GREEN and both are locks, for a reason that is in the test rather than tuned away:** no route-level race can tell the explicit gym-row and upload-row locks apart from the locks Postgres takes anyway — a child insert takes `FOR KEY SHARE` on the `gyms` row, and the confirm updates its own upload row before it commits. Telling them apart needs a seam inside the transaction. The reviewer's note that the lock test's second half cannot separate them is half right: it already leaves the gym row free and holds only the upload row; what it cannot get past is that later UPDATE.

## 2026-09-21 · 3a-iii-b built after a crash mid-card: Press Confirm, and a coin toss in my own code that a test found by failing once

- **The laptop shut down mid-card and nothing was lost.** Every file was on disk, CRLF intact. The previous chat's own scratchpad held what the code did not: its spec drafts, its breaks harness, and the notes of a cost measurement it was applying when the machine died. **Look in the session scratchpads before re-deriving anything** — `analyseEntries` and the cursor fix were both already applied at 07:00, and a tsc+lint run at 07:17 was the last thing it did.
- Re-proved HERE rather than quoted, because last night's numbers were another session's: Verified: api tsc 0 · api eslint src test tools 0 · shared tsc 0 · eslint 0 · shared 193/193 · engine 213/213 · engine purity grep silent · **the whole api suite on the local database and the real Redis 2,270 passed, 87 files, 0 failed** · **26 deliberate breaks with an anchor census first, 24 red**, every file restored identical (sha256). The two green are the gym's row lock and the upload row's `FOR UPDATE`, and they are green for a reason now written into the test rather than tuned away: no route-level race can tell them apart from the locks Postgres takes anyway, since inserting an entry takes `FOR KEY SHARE` on the `gyms` row and the confirm updates its own upload row before it commits.
- **THE FIND, and it is in code this card shipped: `0033` gave the entries table nothing that could say which person came FIRST on a gym's list.** `created_at` defaults to `now()`, the TRANSACTION's clock, so every row one confirm writes carries the same instant and the tie-break fell to `gen_random_uuid()`. Three answers hung on it and all three were random: the spelling shown at the top of a gym's own status chips, the ORDER the chips come in, and which of a household sharing one address is marked "in the app" on the screen staff invite from. **Measured on a copy of the table: of 40 confirms whose file wrote "Active" before "ACTIVE", 16 read the chip back as "ACTIVE".**
- **It was found by this card's own test failing once and passing the next run** — not by reading the code. I first blamed a neighbouring suite, because the run that failed had `orgs.routes.test.ts` beside it; the second pair run was 165/165 and the SQL probe settled it. A coin toss looks exactly like a flake, and the rule's table test could never have caught it: a pure function over an ORDERED array is precisely what the database was failing to be.
- Fixed by migration `0034` — `listed_seq`, a sequence stamped in the file's own row order, with a `(gym_id, listed_seq)` index — and the three queries order by it. A SEQUENCE and not the file's row number, so a second confirm and 3a-iv's typed-in person APPEND rather than restart at one. Two code comments that asserted the old behaviour was correct are struck; one of them named the exact reason it fails.
- **`drizzle-kit generate` cannot be used in this repo and has not been since `0012`:** `drizzle/meta/` stops at `0012_snapshot.json`, so it re-emits 21 migrations and stops on an interactive rename prompt (proved on the untouched schema too, so it is not this card's). `0016`–`0033` are all hand-written and each says so; `0034` follows, journal entry in the same commit.
- **And two tests claimed concurrency guarantees they could not observe.** `app.ts` builds `postgres(url, { max: 1 })`, so two presses of Confirm through ONE app queue on that single connection and apply one after the other whatever the code does — the deliberate break that removed the gym's row lock stayed GREEN. The join race was worse: it `await`ed the confirm before the join started, and raced the APPLY door, which touches no member and no gym row. Both now run across a twin app (a second connection), and the seat-claiming door — staff admitting an application, which is `claimSeat`'s caller — is what races the confirm. Production runs more than one API process, so the lock is the only thing between two presses and a double apply.
- Cost at full size (§4), measured here through the real HTTP server with a bystander stream beside every call, on AC at the full 2,592 MHz: the reads are cheap at BOTH sizes because the kept list is an indexed table — a page of ten thousand names costs a bystander **11-12 ms** and walking all hundred pages **22-25 ms**, where the PREVIEW page of the same people costs 226-334 ms because it cuts a page out of a document. **The confirm is the expensive one: 980-1,635 ms of answering NOBODY at ten thousand**, 183-184 ms at the launch shape. That is the pool of one (Stage 4 item 11), and it is bounded by a gym pressing the button about once a month. The post-confirm `ANALYZE` was re-measured rather than trusted: `membersAgainstList` is 21.6 ms stale and 9.3 ms fresh, the ANALYZE costs 269 ms, and the walk straight after a confirm is 3,198 ms with it against 4,514 ms without — a modest win bought cheaply. **An earlier note in the tree claiming a hundredfold and 3,152 ms is struck: I could not reproduce it.**
- **26 deliberate breaks, 24 red**, anchor census first (25/25 present — the 07:00 edits had rotted none). #19, the cursor left open last night, is RED now. The two green are the two LOCKS, and they are green for a reason rather than a gap: no route-level race can tell an explicit lock apart from the ones Postgres takes anyway, and a confirm of seven people is too short a transaction for two presses to collide even across two connections. Written into the test, with what it would take to pin them — a seam inside the transaction — and why that is not worth it here.
- Next: **review round one in a fresh chat** (the prompt is CLAUDE.md §6). The two extra passes do NOT run on this card — they run once over the whole feature after 3a-iv (RULINGS 2026-09-21). The four dashboard images on disk are still not this card's, and `apps/api/.cost/` is scratch that is deliberately not committed.
## 2026-09-21 · 3a-iii-a: the re-check refused the round — two Highs made by my own fixes, and a cost number that measured the wrong thing

- **Kd lost the reviewer chat in the same crash.** It was not gone: Claude Code keeps every session's transcript, and `claude --resume 9d1d3f36-7434-4b68-a30d-fa6bfd83b98f` reopened "PR #87 member-list-preview audit" with its findings intact. **Look there before ever re-running a review** — and a deleted scratch file is recoverable the same way, out of the `Write` call in the transcript, which is how the cost harness came back.
- Seven of the ten findings closed. Three did not, and two of those were mine, made by the fixes themselves.
- **High-A: `canBeInvited` was being counted by SUBTRACTION on the read** — everybody with an address in the file, minus everybody already in the app. That is the same number only while every member the file reaches also has an address IN it, and §9.7's backup match means one need not: a member is matched by the phone the gym holds. One such row and the upload said `canBeInvited: 0` while a second look at the SAME upload said **-1** — a number `memberListChangeCounts` forbids, which nothing re-parses on the way out, on a screen staff were about to act on. Both answers now come from one pure rule (`inviteCounts`), so the POST and the GET cannot differ.
- **High-B: the statement behind every fact about a gym's own members read the whole list once per member.** An `OR` across two columns rules out both of `0033`'s indexes, and casting `u.email` to `text` rules out the citext one — so 200 members against 10,000 entries cost **207 ms and 51,212 shared buffers**, on the ONE Postgres connection the whole API shares, on the preview read and on every page of names, growing as members × entries. As a `UNION ALL` of two indexed lookups: **4.5 ms and 2,813 buffers** (`EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)`, both ways, same data).
- **And the cast was WRONG, not just slow** — nobody had asked for this one. text-to-text is case-sensitive, so the database answered a different question from `reconcile`'s own `foldEmail`, which folds case: one member could be on the gym's list to the rule and off it to the database. citext to citext is the fast answer and the correct one, and a test drives that case rather than trusting that sign-in lower-cases what it stores today.
- **High-3 (still open): the cost table reported what a bystander TYPICALLY waits**, where §4 asks how long the server answers NOBODY — the longest. The bystander is a back-to-back stream of `GET /health` now, each from a fresh address, and §9.9's table gives median AND worst at both sizes, re-measured at the processor's full 2,592 MHz (Kd plugged the laptop in; the earlier figures were taken at 1,495). **At the launch shape nothing holds the thread above 4 ms and the worst a bystander waits beside a page is 9 ms. At ten thousand a page costs about a third of a second of answering nobody** — unchanged across all three versions of this code, because what it waits on is the pool of one: Stage 4 item 11, and the next thing to fix for this screen.
- Low-7 (still open): the list state was read twice a request, and **three times** on the upload path once `measure` was counted. Read once and threaded through.
- Two new tests, each with its own deliberate break: the phone-matched member with no address in the file (the upload and the re-read must agree, and nothing may go below zero), and a member whose address differs from the list's only in case. The old test's invariant — that the three counts under `new` sum to it — is struck: it is algebraically true of any subtraction, which is exactly why it let High-A through, and it is not true of the rule.
- Verified: api tsc 0 · api eslint src test tools 0 · shared tsc 0 · eslint 0 · shared 186/186 · **the whole api suite on the local database and the real Redis 2,243 passed, 85 files, 0 failed** · **10 deliberate breaks, all 10 red**, with an anchor census first — two anchors had been rotted by these very edits, which a census catches and a green break hides — plus the Low's 2, also red.
- **The two extra passes did NOT run on this card, and that is a rule change, not a skip** (RULINGS 2026-09-21, the chat's call on Kd's question about time). They move from the card to the FEATURE: once over 3a-i to 3a-iv together, after 3a-iv, before any gym touches item 5's screen. Round one is unchanged and stays per card. Measured from the reviewer chat's own transcript: round one 24 minutes, its re-checks 14 and 10.
- **The round then CLOSED in that same chat**: all four reproduced, both measurements reproduced with the record's own instrument, nothing Critical/High open. It left ONE Low, fixed here without a further re-check (RULINGS 2026-09-16): the two match channels were joined but never ORDERED, so the database took whichever entry was oldest, where §9.7 and `entryFor` take the verified ADDRESS first and the phone only as the backup. A member with a newer address entry and an older phone entry was shown the older one's status word and member number — "was Frozen, OLD-1" about somebody the list calls "Active, NEW-2". It is only that sentence, never who is on the list and never a count, but it is false about a named person. ## 2026-09-21 · 3a-iii-a: PR #87's review round one fixed (3 High, 5 Low, 4 weak tests), after a crash mid-commit

- **The laptop shut down while the previous chat was writing its handoff and committing.** Nothing was lost: every fix was on disk, CRLF intact, no half-applied break. All of it was re-proved in this chat rather than quoted — the checks, the whole suite, the eight breaks and the cost table were each run again here.
- Both Highs were made by the paging fix of the night before, and one fix closes both.
- **High-1: the fast path served stale answers ABOUT PEOPLE.** Freshness was decided by the list's version, and nothing about a member joining, proving an address, being given a phone number or leaving moves that version. Driven: three people staged at 10:00 (`alreadyInApp 0 · canBeInvited 3`); Amara Okafor joins and verifies at 10:05; at 10:06 the same preview still said `alreadyInApp 0` and the names behind it still said "Amara Okafor — in the app: false", and would have for the preview's whole hour — staff offered an invite for somebody already here.
- **High-2: a member's own name, PROVED email and stated phone were written into `gym_member_list_uploads.rows`** — a table this card's own comment declares holds no user's data, that the Day-14 purge does not touch and that a person's export does not carry. Read straight out of the column after an upload whose file held neither value.
- **The class fix: NO FACT ABOUT A MEMBER IS EVER STORED.** The stored grouping holds only what the FILE and the LIST say; in-app, seats, who is leaving and who they are are worked out again on every read from two small statements through one pure rule the stage uses too. That cannot go stale by construction; the reviewer's own suggestion (a fingerprint of the members' table) would have missed email verification and a stated phone number, and missed them silently.
- **High-3: the "cost at full size" numbers were the event loop, not the server.** The API's Postgres pool is ONE connection, so a request waiting on the database is one nothing else gets past; the reviewer measured a bystander's `/health` waiting 309 ms beside a page. Re-measured here through the real HTTP server with a bystander beside every call, three runs at 200 and two at 10,000 — spec §9.9 carries the table. A bystander now waits 8–17 ms beside every read. **The numbers are about twice the night's, because the laptop was on battery and its processor held at 1,495 of 2,592 MHz; that is stated beside the table.** The pool itself is ROADMAP Stage 4 item 11.
- Lows, all five: THREE shipped sentences claimed a preview is always computed afresh — one in spec §9.9, two in this card's ROADMAP line — and all three are struck; neither read route had the validation-failure test §4 requires (a table of nine now); an identity key travelled in an Error message Sentry captures (gone); `listState` was read twice in one request (hoisted); and the 352 new lines of `@app/shared` the review counted had no test of their own, which they now have — the base64 ceiling, the cursor, the guard's edges and the words a gone upload is described with.
- Weak tests, all four: the staleness test has the companion it was missing (a member joining DOES change the answer); the leak capture's control was satisfied by the server's boot lines and is now a request-scoped line; the two gates ran only on the in-memory Redis and now have `memberList.realRedis.test.ts` on the real one, which CI runs, with a random front-desk address per run because a real Redis keeps its counters for the window's whole hour; and the version-bump test's limit is recorded — nothing in this half moves the version, so 3a-iii-b's confirm is what will reach that path for real.
- **A test-suite fault of its own, NOT this card's, found by running the whole suite twice here.** `auth.routes.test.ts` deleted every `p21-%` user, which is also `workouts.sync.test.ts`'s `p21-sync-%`; the two files run at the same moment, so whichever got there first destroyed the other. Seen both ways round — once ten sync tests failing 401 with their users deleted mid-test, once this suite's own delete failing on the workouts foreign key the sync suite had just written. CI would have met it about half the time. The pattern now excludes the other suite's people; proved in the database, and the two files pass run together.

## 2026-09-20 · 3a-iii-a: Kd asked what breaks at scale and what leaks — one real fault in my own code

- Kd, after the card was green and pushed: think of scenarios, and he gave two shapes as examples — *"working fine in 10 people but compltely crashes as soon as there is a million user"* and *"a person sign in but there application gets leak"*. Both were measured, not opined on. **He also said twice that my answers were unreadable**; the second attempt was a plain list of what could hurt a person, which is what he wanted.
- **THE FAULT, in code I had just shipped.** A page of a hundred names re-read the whole file and re-ran the whole comparison: on a 10,000-person gym one page blocked the request thread for **121 ms**, the same whichever page it was, so walking the list blocked the server for about **twelve seconds in total** — against the **14–15 ms** 3a-ii moved the file read into a worker to protect. Exactly Kd's "fine at ten, dead at a million".
- Fixed by storing the grouping beside the rows when the file is staged, and cutting a page out of the document in the DATABASE (`jsonb_array_elements` + `WITH ORDINALITY`). Neither read touches the gym's list. A page now blocks for **11 ms**, reading the preview back **3 ms** (was 84), the upload **78 ms** (was 116). Not a cache that can be wrong: the stored answer is the answer at `base_version`, and a gym that has moved its list since gets the whole comparison again — the expensive path, now the rare one.
- Two limits recorded rather than fixed: a preview still blocks 78 ms at ten thousand people (the rule runs on the request thread and cannot move to the worker, which has no database); and `postgres.js` refuses more than 65,534 parameters, so **3a-iii-b's confirm must use `jsonb_to_recordset`** or it fails at about 8,000 people — measured, which is why the spec's shape is not a style choice.
- **The leak half: two things §9.10 asks for that I had skipped now exist.** `memberList.leak.test.ts` builds the app at `trace` with request logging ON and captures every byte pino writes through a good upload, a refusal, an unmappable file, a page of names, and a fault carrying the file as its body — no name, address, phone, member number, status word or file body appears. `sentry.test.ts` now sends a member file's bytes through its forced fault beside the photo. Both mutation-tested (logging the first three people of a file turns the capture red).
- **One real hazard, and it lands on 3a-iii-b:** Postgres puts the WHOLE failing row into a CHECK violation's `detail` — measured printing a person's name, email and phone — and nothing strips it before an error is logged or sent to Sentry. Nothing in THIS slice can draw one (every CHECK here is on a value the server computes, and a file with a NUL byte is refused by 3a-i before the database sees it). The confirm's INSERT of entries can. Fix: strip `detail` in one place before anything logs an error.
- Verified: api tsc 0 · api eslint src test tools 0 · shared tsc 0 · eslint 0 · shared 160/160 · **the whole api suite on the local database 2,224 passed, 84 files, 0 failed**. 6 more deliberate breaks: 4 red at once; of the two green, one was my breaks script's own bad anchor (red when applied by hand) and the other is genuinely unobservable through a route — the rule's duplicate guard, which 3a-ii's reader already makes unreachable, so the unit test pins the boundary instead of claiming the guard.
- **Kd then asked whether there were other bugs, and a fresh read of the new paging code found two — both in paths nothing had exercised.** `jsonb_array_length` of a group key that is not there answers SQL NULL rather than an error, so a document missing a group would have sent `total: null` to a screen and broken the reply's own contract silently; the total is parsed now, like everything else read back out of the database. And `gone` and `members_leaving` had never been paged through the ROUTE — they are the two groups whose people are not rows of the file, read by a different statement, and `members_leaving` is the one group whose name on the wire differs from the key it is stored under. Both are covered now by one test that pages all five groups and asserts every page's total is the number the preview showed; breaking the key mapping and breaking the branch each turn it red.
- **Still open, and they are Kd's to decide, not bugs:** a typo in a gym's spreadsheet invites a STRANGER into the gym (nothing checks an address belongs to the person named beside it); a person who asks us to delete their data cannot have the gym's list entry removed; somebody who quit the app deliberately is offered for invite again next month; the big-change alarm never fires below 10 people, which is most small gyms; and nothing records which member of staff read which names. All four belong to 3b's plan (the invites), not to this card.
- **CI caught one thing the local full suite did not, and it is the shared-database class again.** My fixture plan was in GBP because the fixture gym is British — and `orgs.routes.test.ts` proves a gym in a currency with no price book is refused rather than guessed at, picking GBP and checking first that GBP is unseeded. Seeding it turned that suite red on CI while everything passed here, because the order the two run in decides whether my plan exists yet. Both member-list suites now seed INR, with the reason written above the constant. **A fixture plan's currency is not free: it is a row another suite may be asserting the absence of.**

Reading the list needs `members.confirm`, never `members.read` — it holds the addresses of people who never opened the app.
- Settled while building, each now a test: the rate limiter is called INSIDE the handler after the privilege gate (CLAUDE.md §4's order, against this module's habit), so a stranger's 404 and a trainer's 403 never spend the front desk's allowance; `ipMax` is explicit on both limiters and driven at ONE fixed address; a status word is compared with case folded, and the spelling SHOWN is the file's own first row; `leaving` is "on the list now and not on the new one", so somebody who dropped off months ago is not counted again for ever; a member is matched to an ENTRY, so a family sharing one address is answered right; a preview past its hour is expired by the clock whether or not the hourly job has run.
