## 2026-09-20 · 3a-ii: Kd's five questions, measured — a card number we must not keep, and what happens when gyms upload at once

- Kd asked whether the new rule costs time, whether anything else is wrong, what happens when a gym uses different English words, what happens when several gyms upload at the same moment, and whether a fault like this could be sitting in what is already built. Four of the five were answered by running things, not by opinion.
- **Found and fixed: a bank card number would have been kept.** Gym software calls a member's door fob a "card number", so that heading is read as a member number (the spec's own list) — but an export whose "Card Number" really is a payment card would have left its digits in our list. A number shaped like a card AND carrying a card's check digit (Luhn, 13–19 digits) is now dropped: the person keeps their name, email and phone, and we hold nothing worth stealing. The 16-digit member number in our own fixture is not card-shaped and survives.
- **Different English words, measured over 37 real headings**: most are already read (Mail, E-Mail ID, Contact Email, Cell, Cell #, Phone No, Daytime Phone, Given Name, Christian Name, Forename, Surname, Membership State). Missed and now added: Tel, Contact No, Membership No, Client Number, Customer Number. Deliberately NOT added: Account Number and any bank-sounding word. And a heading nobody has listed is still rescued by the cells — "Username" over real addresses is read as the email column on its values alone.
- Scenarios run: the headings repeated every few rows (the repeat is skipped with a reason, the people are kept); the columns swapped, names under Email (nothing is guessed, staff are asked); "Email Opt-In" beside "Email" (the real one wins, and the opt-in is never the membership's status); an unrecognised heading over real addresses (taken on its cells). **Still open, and honestly a limit**: where the ONLY address column in a file belongs to somebody else under a word no list has ("Buddy Email"), it is used. What closes that is not a longer list but the preview — nobody is emailed until staff press Invite (§9.2 rule 10), and the column's heading and three of its cells are on that screen. Item 5's acceptance test is now: can staff see whose address is about to be invited.
- **Several gyms at once, measured on Node 22.23.2** with the biggest file allowed (10,000 × 30) uploaded by 1, 2, 3 and 5 gyms at the same moment: 2 are read at once, the rest are told "Other files are being read right now. Try again in a minute." Nothing collapses, nothing crosses between gyms, the API's own thread stalls at most 54 ms and the whole process stays at 123–134 MB. The cap is what keeps the server standing; when there are many gyms the answer is more capacity, not a bigger cap on one box.
- 2 more deliberate breaks, both red (the card guard, and its check digit). Verified: api tsc 0 · api eslint src test tools 0 · memberList 712/712.
- Open for Kd: whether the "name the worst thing this can do to a person, and test that first" rule goes into CLAUDE.md, and whether a chat should sweep the word lists already shipped (the allergen list first — a missed allergen is a health answer, and the photo word lists took three rounds of patches in August).

## 2026-09-20 · 3a-ii: whose email gets invited — two faults Kd's question found, and English only

- Kd, after the round closed: *"is there anything bug here beacuse this is the most critical feature of the application gyms uploads document and applicatio extract information and send invites to user"*. He is right about the stakes: the gym's invite is its yes (§9.2 rule 11), so an address read from the wrong column joins a stranger to the gym. Ran 37 real headings that name somebody who is NOT the member through the reader.
- **25 of the 37 were read as the member's own** — nominee, father, mother, husband, wife, son, daughter, brother, sister, relative, family member, friend, guarantor, sponsor, reference, witness, attendant, caretaker, carer, doctor, physician, physio, therapist, agent, broker, manager, contact person, corporate, kin — all are on the never-list now, and 0 of 37 are read as the member's. A column refused that way is still shown, with what its heading claimed (`headerSays`).
- **"Nominee Email" outranked "Email"** and became the column every member would have been invited at: the rank read the WORD matched, not the heading. Now a heading that IS its word beats one that merely HOLDS it — "Zzyzx Email" ranks after "Email" too, so it holds for a qualifier no list has ever heard of. That is the class fix; a never-list alone can never be complete.
- Kd in the same message: **the file is read in ENGLISH only, and the market is English-speaking Western countries, not India** (RULINGS 2026-09-20). The German, French, Spanish, Portuguese, Dutch and Italian heading words are struck. A file whose headings are in another language is not refused and nothing of it is guessed wrong — its columns come back unmapped for staff to map. The PEOPLE are untouched: José Álvarez and Zoë Müller are read, matched and invited like anyone else, and every country's phone numbers are still read.
- Shown on a hostile but ordinary English export (a title block, "Emergency Contact Email" beside "Email", "Payment Status" beside "Membership Status", the front desk's address on 6 rows, one member twice, an Excel-shortened mobile, a totals row, one member with nothing): Mary Johnson, whose own email cell is empty, comes out with no email rather than her emergency contact's; the front-desk address is dropped from 6 rows and named in the words; John Williams falls back to his home phone; the totals row and the empty member are skipped with a reason.
- Not a fault, and worth knowing: the same person on two membership rows (two member numbers) is two entries, because the member number is part of who a row is (§9.5) — the gym's own file says they are two records, and `shared_emails` says they share an address. And a family of six sharing one address loses it to the placeholder rule (more than 5 rows), which is said in words with the address named.
- 3 more deliberate breaks, all red: the never-list, the ranking, and a non-English word put back. Verified: api tsc 0 · api eslint src test tools 0 · memberList 704/704 · whole api suite without a database 1,346 passed, 808 skipped · shared 160/160.

## 2026-09-20 · 3a-ii: PR #86's review round one fixed (1 Critical, 1 High, 3 Low, 3 weak tests)

- Both big findings reproduced here before anything was touched. **C1:** the reader answered any number the phone package calls "possible", which is wider than the E.164 shape the row schema and 3a-iii's column both hold — measured here: `030 123456789012` under a German gym reads as 16 digits, Gibraltar's `008153942982595231308` as 19, `+4904243` as 6. One such cell made the whole file fail its own contract in the request's thread: a 500 for 3a-iii, no row number, and nine thousand good members lost to row 4,000.
- C1 fixed at the reader, not the contract (widening it would store 19-digit strings and break matching in 3a-iii): `MEMBER_LIST_PHONE_E164` is now the ONE definition, used by `readPhone` and by `memberListRowSchema`, and a cell it refuses falls through to the row's next phone column, which keeps its email.
- **H1:** `too_many_rows` is drawn by two kinds of file. The grid counts blank rows against its row cap, so a list of 5,011 people with a blank row after each one trips the cut and was told it "holds more than 10,000 people" — false, and the advice (split it) was wrong too. Reproduced through the tool on a 5,011-person file; the sentence is now true of both: "This sheet is longer than we can read. A member list may hold 10,000 people: take out any blank rows between them, or split the file…".
- Lows: an unnamed sheet in the ignored-sheets warning was numbered by its place in what is left, not in the workbook (unreachable today, wrong the moment a file has one); a comment claimed Excel writes U+000B for Alt+Enter — it writes CRLF, checked in the fixture — and U+000B and U+000C are now line breaks rather than dropped characters, so two numbers written one above the other are not glued; and every column now carries `headerSays`, what its heading claimed, so a screen can tell staff a column named Email was read and disbelieved.
- The three weak tests: the "never the file's cells" assertion could not fail (Zod strips unknown keys), so a seam reports what the worker really posted and the test reads that — no grid, nothing from the ignored sheet, and at most the three samples of the joining-date column; the phone contract was only ever checked against hand-written rows, so there is now a sweep over all 254 countries and 16 lengths asserting every number read fits the one shape (1,000+ read); and the blank-row refusal had no test at all.
- 6 more deliberate breaks, one per fix: all 6 turned their own test red, every file restored identical (sha256). The vertical-tab break was green until its own two cases were added to the phone table.

- The reviewer's own numbers for the record, measured on Node 24 in its chat: 37,260 fuzzed cells, 5,029 read, 157 outside the contract before the fix; whole read 915–970 ms with an 11.5–12.1 ms stall, which makes the spec's Node 22 figures conservative rather than wrong.

## 2026-09-20 · 3a-ii built: understanding the file (branch `member-file-understand`)

- Kd: *"go"* to the plan (Opus, effort set to max). Server only — no route, no database, no screen. `packages/shared/src/memberList.ts` gains the mapping, the column, the row, the skipped and the warning shapes with their words and every constant; `apps/api/src/modules/orgs/memberList/` gains `cells`, `headerWords`, `columns`, `fields`, `phone` and `understand`; `tools/read-member-file.ts <path> --country=IN [--show=5]` prints what the server made of a file on disk.
- **It runs in the worker.** Measured first on the request thread: understanding the biggest list allowed (10,000 × 30) costs 559–634 ms on Node 22.23.2, which the API would answer nothing else during — 3a-i had kept that thread to a 19–24 ms stall. So `workerAnswer` understands the grid where it opened it and posts the ROWS; `understandMemberFile(bytes, { country, mapping, remembered })` is what 3a-iii calls, `parseMemberFile(bytes)` is unchanged. Re-measured: 1,328–1,398 ms all told, stall 14–15 ms, RSS 96 → 130 MB, peak 203–234 MB.
- Found building, each now a table test: a joining date IS a possible phone number to the package (`2024/01/05` is a valid German landline), so a date or a time is never a phone value — without it a headerless file's date column becomes its phone column; a gym with NO country set lost its phone column altogether (2 of 7 cells parsed, under the 0.6 share) and was never told to set one, so the column is found by shape there and only `+` numbers are read; the heading fingerprint is taken from the heading ROW, so a remembered mapping is tried before any guess — the gym whose headings we cannot read is the one that mapped them by hand; Excel's ANSI export wrote `अमित` as `????` with no letter beside the marks, so a `?` anywhere in a name is counted; `Ann Lee <ann@gym.com>` counts as an email cell, a note holding an address does not.
- Refusals added to the shared vocabulary: `too_many_rows`, `too_many_columns`, `no_rows` (3a-i cuts a sheet at its limit and marks it; the cut is refused here) and `mapped_column_not_status`, which carries the column staff chose. Every sentence still says what to do and still never speaks of the file's name — the shared test that pins that now counts 21 refusals.
- Kept to the spec's rules and their reasons: a heading is a hint and the column's own cells decide (0.6 with a heading, 0.8 without); an emergency contact, a trainer or a referrer is never the member; a member number and a status are never guessed from values; the status stays in the gym's own word; the identity key holds the name, email, phone and member number and NOT the status, so "Active" → "Expired" changes a person in place.
- Tests, all written before review: the phone table (111 cases — the 52 vectors of 2026-09-19 re-run on this package, plus every spreadsheet-damage row), the heading words and column rules (116), the four fields and the identity key (96), whole grids (47), the real Excel, Google Sheets and CSV exports carried through to rows and through the real worker (15), and the shared contract (21 more).
- 37 deliberate breaks: 36 turned their own test red. The one that stayed green is the email length check before the pattern runs — a cost guard, not a rule, since sign-in's own `max(254)` gives the same answer; its test now pins the 254 boundary instead of claiming the guard. Four breaks were green at first and are red now: they found the missing date rule, the header-row penalty was unpinned, `extract: false` was unpinned (a flat number in an address), and a name written before its address was not counted. Every file restored identical (sha256).

- H2, L1 and the class: every refusal sentence is now true of every file that draws it — no sentence names the file (the server never sees it), OpenDocument, iWork and the compound file each worded for every program that makes them, `unsafe_archive` no longer says "Excel file". L2: spec says the bytes are copied once and the copy transferred.

- Measured here, not read: `read-excel-file` 9.3.10 believes an Excel file's own size claims — a 139-byte file took the process from 46 MB to 447 MB, a 153 KB one inflated to 150 MB. So our own bounded reader (Node's `zlib`, `maxOutputLength`) unzips and rebuilds the file before the package sees it (§9.4). With `parseNumber: (s) => s` the package keeps `919876543210` exact.
- Measured on the Excel 16 on this machine (COM automation works; Python 3.14 is here, LibreOffice is not): "CSV" is Windows-1252 and turns Ł and Devanagari into `?`; "CSV UTF-8" carries the mark; "Unicode Text" is UTF-16 and TAB; "CSV (Macintosh)" is Mac Roman with bare CR; every CSV writes a 12-digit phone as `9.19877E+11`. `TextDecoder` has `windows-1252`, `macintosh`, `utf-16le`, not `ibm850`. - The design's spine: the list table is exactly the newest confirmed list; matching is worked out when asked (verified email, else the phone the member gave) and never stored; history is one timestamp on the membership; one pure `reconcile` rule feeds the preview, the confirm, the reads and the removal; a change over `max(10, 10 %)` needs a tick on that one request; nothing but name, email, phone and member number is ever kept.

A person joins by email only where the gym INVITED that address. NOT taken: fees, renewals, payments (§9.11) — his to reopen.

## 2026-09-18 · 7a-iv-e built: your own ring numbers (branch `own-ring-numbers`)

- Kd: *"go"* to the plan (Opus xhigh, as it recommended). What a person sees: a switch over the Nutrition rings — **App's plan · My own** — each side showing its own calories; "My own" opens a box filled from the app's numbers, and the four it saves feed the rings, "Remaining today" and its "over" rows. Switching back keeps what was typed; one tap sends it again. "Gym's plan" is not shown, having nothing behind it yet (Stage 2 item 10).
- Server: migration `0032` adds `user_nutrition_targets` — one row per person, written only when the switch is touched, so an absent row IS the app's plan and nothing changes for anyone who never opens it. Its CHECKs are rails, not the rule (the source, all four numbers or none, 'own' never without them, 0–20,000 kcal, 0–2,000 g): the health rule depends on answers that move under a stored row, so freezing it into the table would be a second, staler answer.
- `PUT /v1/nutrition/targets` takes `{source:"app"}` or `{source:"own", kcal, proteinG, carbsG, fatG}` and answers with the whole targets payload, so the rings and the switch cannot disagree. The contract now carries both sets, which one is showing and why a stored set is not — `appTargets`, `own`, `source`, `ownHeld` — with refines that refuse every impossible pairing ("My own" lit while the rings draw the app's plan is the one nobody would notice on screen).
- THE RULE (`ownTargetsHeld`, `nutrition/targets.ts`): never below the app's 1,200 kcal floor, and never below what keeps the weight for a plan that holds no cut — read from the plan's own no-cut REASONS (`getUserPlanAndCutRules`), not its `no_deficit` flag, which is only raised on a "lose" plan; a 17-year-old who picked "keep my weight" is refused at the same figure. The higher bound binds and is the one named.
- It runs on the way in AND on every read, because the answers under a number move (a health yes, a birthday, a heavier body): a set allowed in March stops feeding the rings once the health question is answered yes in June. The numbers are never rewritten or dropped — the app's plan shows, and the screen says which figure holds them. A plan still missing an answer holds them too (`plan_incomplete`), said beside the questions on the honest empty state.
- Settled while building, on the plan Kd said go to (RULINGS 2026-09-18 clause): the macros are typed freely and the box says what they come to ("… come to 1,940 kcal"), never rewriting one of the four to match another. A wrong-side target blanks the APP's numbers, not the person's own — the switch is the way out of a stale target.
- Privacy: the table is on both lists, deleted at Day 14 (`privacy/repo.ts`) and in the export, on `user_fitness_profiles`' footing; both privacy suites seed a row, so a forgotten DELETE or reader goes red.
- Tests: the rule as ONE table before review (20 classes — both bounds, the burn under the floor, every reason); the contract's impossible pairings; the route end to end (each refusal by name, the switch back keeping the numbers, the health answer changing under a stored set, the under-18 hold, tenancy over two people, one row each); migration `0032`'s rails by causing each and its cascade; and the screen's wiring end to end (7 cases). The ring test runs on an app of its own — two more sign-ups pass the per-address limit this file shares.

## 2026-09-17 · Decided: the app sits beside the gym's software (records only, no code; branch `records-beside-the-gym`)

- Kd asked how a gym's own entry system (fingerprint, face, card, QR) connects to the app, in India and abroad. Three web checks (India, abroad, the law) and two device manuals read; the findings are on RULINGS 2026-09-17 and ROADMAP's "After launch".
- His second question: should we build it at all, and what about the uploaded list going stale, a QR nobody will scan, the leaderboard and the cheer resting on attendance, manual work for the gym, and cards split that belong together. He asked for engineering judgement, not agreement, and said earlier chats patched and deferred to old rulings.
- Answer given and adopted (*"yes adopt it, update rulings and roadmap"*): the gym's software is the source of truth, ours reconciles from its uploads and never gates (a source of truth is not a gatekeeper); the join code admits at once; one "active day" definition feeds the leaderboard, "slipping away" and the cheer, and a workout counts on its own; phone check-in comes with the phone app; door machines after launch on request (eSSL/ZKTeco first), never a fingerprint or face.
- Records: RULINGS — seven new 2026-09-17 lines (the model, the reconciling list, no manual per-member work, active day, attendance sources, the research, and two process lines under "How work is done"); amendments on 2026-08-19 (applications), 2026-08-24 (reaffirmed), 2026-08-31 and 2026-09-02 (workouts count on their own). ROADMAP — Stage 2 reshaped (1a/1b, 2 re-pointed, 3 and 5 as one design), Stage 5 item 8 (phone check-in), an entry-systems bullet under "After launch".
- Verified: nothing to run; a records-only push runs the secrets scan only.
- Open: the lawyer review (Stage 4 item 3) must confirm that receiving a number and a time keeps the app outside biometric law. The next chat builds Stage 2 item 3, the member list's server half.

## 2026-09-16 · 7a-iv-a built: adding a food by measure (branch `food-measures`)

- Kd, shown 7a-iv's plan, ruled the portion redesigned on the professional apps' model (RULINGS 2026-09-16, from two research agents reading MyFitnessPal, Cronometer, MacroFactor, Lose It!, Yazio, Cal AI, SnapCalorie and Foodvisor's own help pages): every food carries its measures, a photo row starts at one only within 30 % of the photo's grams, three pull requests. *"Go, all three (Recommended)"*. ROADMAP 7a-iv → 7a-iv-a, -b, -c.
- Measured for the plan: on his eight plates the model writes a count of 1 for almost every food (mashed avocado, brie, strawberries); within 3× his scrambled eggs, 100 g, became one large egg, 61 g, and a mug of iced coffee, 250 g, USDA's "medium", 496 g.
- Server: the search gives each food its measures and where it starts (`measures.ts`: USDA's household measures named by the importer's rule, moved here; our list reads the entry it cites; its own serving where no USDA measure of its name weighs within a tenth of it — our blueberry muffin, sourdough slice, chocolate chip cookie and protein bar keep theirs beside USDA's other size; a label's serving; g and oz). Every food starts at its serving (swept on the local table: 0 of 13,225 USDA foods and 0 of our 318 start elsewhere). No measure is made of a USDA row of amount 0 whose text states none (frozen kale's 94 g "package (10 oz)") or a survey "Guideline amount" row, and the importer's serving skips them too (local re-import: kale serves by "0.33 cup", 67 g); a name over 40 characters is cut at a clause end only where 20 stay, and never leaves a bracket open; an "oz" serving starts at one ounce only when it weighs one. An item may be `{canonical, measure, amount}`: weighed from the server's list (`unknown_measure`, `portion_out_of_range`), stored as `measure {id, name, amount}` (no migration), kept by an edit that sends the same grams (a food twice in one meal: each item by its place). A saved dish weighs by the food's own "cup" or "half cup", else 1 g a ml, and is stored as a measure; a scan never applies a saved dish (RULINGS 2026-07-18: a saved Medium bowl was used for any "katori").
- Web: `MeasurePicker` in Add food and "Add an ingredient" (saved meal, photo sheet): − / + by half (ten grams), typed amounts, the food's measures then saved dishes, "How full?" for a dish, "Save a new dish…" at Appendix B's global sizes (cup 240, mug 325, bowl 375 ml, or typed); a saved meal reads "Apple: 1.5 × medium (3" dia), 273g". ## 2026-09-16 · PR #72's second re-check fixed (1 Critical, 1 High, 2 Low, 4 weak tests); a re-check of these fixes next

- Critical: @sentry/node 10.63 sent a request's body (first 10,000 characters), headers, cookies and query string, and each outgoing call's query string as a breadcrumb, whatever sendDefaultPii says. `src/sentry.ts` (the API and the worker) keeps no body and takes the request and the breadcrumbs off every event. The review's fix left the breadcrumbs: a food search's words still went (measured).
- High: a scan that fails for anything but the photo (an outage, a refusal, a fault, one after the model answered too) gives back what it took, on the server: the scan it counted (`refundQuota`, a Lua decrement that never goes below zero nor makes a key), or a new free retry for a scan that rode one. Three failures in five minutes pause that person's scans in `validateScan`, before a free retry is spent or a scan counted. The review's fix (a free retry per failure) was not enough: that token lives in the open photo sheet for ten minutes, so closing the sheet still lost the scan.
- The failure that starts the pause says "unavailable", not "in a minute"; the pause is shorter than a free retry lasts, so one given back as it begins outlives it.
- Web: a 503 keeps the free retry held unless it brings a new one; a 400 `invalid_retake` drops it: "That free retry has run out. Pick the photo again."
- Lows: a word that says there is none is never named under "Not in the total" (an item's reads as its hint); "The review's case:" gone from the test.
- Tests: what Sentry is sent, through the real SDK on a real port with a recording transport (red on the old options: all seven kinds of request data went); the pause at 4:59.999 and 5:00 in literal minutes; the web's free retry through every answer; the prompt's no-value words read from the prompt.
- Verified: api tsc 0 · eslint 0 · nutrition unit 35/35 · entitlements unit 12/12 · nutrition routes 47/47 + sentry 1/1 on local Postgres · full local 1070/1070 (the run before: 1050 passed and `auth.routes`' setup failed at its `p21-%` user delete; 20/20 alone; ROADMAP 10) · shared tsc 0 · eslint 0 · 106/106 · web 47/47 in the five files that draw Nutrition (its 7 old lint errors, none on changed lines) · the Lua on the local Redis · 24 deliberate breaks each red, files restored identical.
- Next: CI, the fresh-chat re-check of these fixes only, merge; then 7a-iii.

## 2026-09-16 · PR #72's re-check fixed (1 High, 5 Low, 4 weak tests); a re-check of these fixes next

- High: an outage's free retry never ran out and every HTTP error was an outage, so a photo Google answers 400 bought endless provider calls for one scan. Now only 408, 429, a 5xx, the network, the timeout or a reply that is not the provider's is an outage, with a free retry at most 3 times per person in 10 minutes (`MAX_OUTAGE_RETRIES`, counted in Redis, fail closed); any other status is a refusal: 503 `nutrition_unavailable`, no free retry, logged as an error.
- A fault (anything thrown that is not a `VisionProviderError`) is answered like a refusal, since a retry meets the same fault (it was "busy" with a free retry); it is logged with its error and sent to Sentry with the request id through app.ts's `reportError`, which the central error handler now uses too (L4).
- Only the model's answer carries usage, and the error's constructor enforces it, so a reply that is not the provider's writes no zero-token ledger row (DECISIONS 2026-07-12: no completion, no row). Every `nutrition.scan_failed` line carries the request id.
- Lows: "unknown" is no name for a meal, container or size (`NO_VALUE_WORDS`); Groq's empty or null content is "unreadable" with its usage; ROADMAP 7a-i's count line and item 7's line (what the scanner does, not the review story).
- Web: a 503 other than "busy" reads "Try again later · Meal scanning is unavailable right now." with no free retry (it fell to the generic error toast).
- Tests: the chain ends at the third free retry, per person, and starts again after 10 minutes; the review's case through the real Gemini adapter (a fetch answering 400: five tries 503, 503, 429, 429, 429 and 2 provider calls); a real 429 and a broken reply ledger nothing; a fault reaches Sentry with the response's request id; HTTP 300–599 on both providers; empty answers on both; 503 `nutrition_unavailable` and `quota_unavailable` never say busy.
- Verified: api tsc 0 · eslint 0 · nutrition unit 35/35 · full local 1065/1065 · shared tsc 0 · eslint 0 · 106/106 · web 49/49 in the six files that draw Nutrition (its 7 old lint errors, none on changed lines); every new test red before the fix; 15 deliberate breaks each red (the review's "any 503 is busy" among them), files restored identical.

## 2026-09-11 · One daily-calories number: the rings read the plan (item 4a-iii), branch `rings-read-the-plan`

- Kd rejected the protein question as first framed (cap at 35 % or keep) and asked for the science. Researched
  (ISSN 2017, ACSM/AND/DC 2016, Longland 2016, Devries 2018, Weijs 2025): grams per kilo, never a share of the calories;
  a body over BMI 30 is counted at its BMI-30 weight (120 kg at 175 cm: 184 g, not 240). RULINGS 2026-09-11.

  use (old −400/+300 calculator retired). Empty rings name the open questions; "Answer now" → onboarding's first open

## 2026-09-08 · The plan maths and its sanity rules (Stage 1 item 3a), branch `plan-maths`

- Built: the contract `packages/shared/src/plan.ts` (answers so far in, `plan` or `missing` out,
  never both) and the pure calculator `apps/api/src/modules/plan/maths.ts`: resting burn
  (Mifflin-St Jeor, as the nutrition targets), daily burn from the day's factor plus the week's
  training, pace → cut or surplus, macros (the targets' protein table, 25 % fat, 50 g carb floor),
  finish date from a "today" the caller passes (no clock). Flags: target wrong direction ·
  target below the healthy weight (BMI 18.5; the plan runs to the floor) · over a year (with the
  gentlest pace that fits, or null) · calorie floor 1200 applied · no deficit (under 18,
  pregnancy, heart, blood pressure, diabetes). Health unanswered = no condition rule yet.
- Engineering choices: day factors are the no-exercise ones (1.2 / 1.3 / 1.45 / 1.6) because
  training is added on top at 5 MET; paces 0.25 / 0.5 / 0.75 kg a week, the same table for a gain.
- Verified: shared + api tsc and eslint exit 0; `test/plan.unit.test.ts` 36 passed; shared 59 passed.
  `tools/plan-preview.ts` prints the numbers for one person (the click-through). No screen, no
  route, no migration — 4a builds the route and screens on this contract.
- Review round 1 (fresh chat): 4 High + 7 Low + 5 test gaps, all fixed in the second commit. A target
  the calories cannot reach (the floor left no cut, or more than ten years away) is now the flag
  `target_out_of_reach` with no date, instead of a crash or a finish date in the year 4417; the
  screen's numbers subtract and add up exactly (change = eat − burn on whole kcal; the macro grams
  make the calories, protein giving way to the 50 g carb floor for a heavy body); one rounded
  healthy-weight floor; two-decimal rails and a bounded start day in the contract; the profile's
  age rail is 16 (RULINGS 2026-09-07) on the server and the old web form; the nutrition targets
  now read resting burn, the floor and the macro split from the plan calculator (the activity factor
  and the cut still differ — moved to 4a in ROADMAP); the preview tool's health screen is unanswered
  unless all four answers are given. Safe mode's `safe_mode` reason noted on 3b.
- Verified after the fixes: shared + api tsc and eslint exit 0; `plan.unit` 44 + `nutrition.unit` 28
  passed; shared 59 passed; `users.fitness.routes` 10 passed on local Postgres.
- A live screen moved: the macro rings' protein gram now gives way to the 50 g carb floor, so a
  very short, very heavy or very old profile (the reviewer measured about 0.5 % of a 912 912-profile
  grid; largest change 242 g → 182 g) shows a smaller protein number than before. It was a bug — the
  old number did not fit in the day's calories — not a ruling change.
- Re-check (fresh chat): no Critical/High; three Lows and three test gaps, fixed in the third commit:
  the flag docstring says when the plan holds the weight instead of running to the floor; resting
  burn takes the `Gender` enum again (the nutrition targets narrow their string once, failing loud);
  the no-clock test compares every field under two clocks; the gain-floor test asserts the flag; and
  a grid sweep over the schema's corners (every goal, both formulas, the age/height/weight rails,
  targets on both sides and past the horizon) checks every plan adds up and the contract accepts it.
- Merged as PR #56 on 2026-09-09 (CI green; branch deleted). On the way, the attendance boundary
  test was fixed to never sit on the gym's midnight (CI had run at 00:00 Kolkata and the test's
  own premise failed before the code ran). Kd merged on the re-check's verdict: no Critical/High.
- Open: 3b (health screening, Safe mode, consent log).
